We are starting the formal implementation of the approved ContentGuard Requirements Validator v2 test plan for PR #11.

The Testing Lead has reviewed the revised test plan in Issue #24.

For this task, keep `scripts/validate_requirements.py` as the validator under test, and create the new permanent pytest suite in:

`test/offline/test_validate_requirements.py`

### Stage 1: Inspect the local project

Read these local files:

- `docs/req/req-schema.json`
- `docs/req/requirements.yaml`
- `scripts/validate_requirements.py`
- `test/offline/`

Check the current Git branch and working-tree status.

Do not access GitHub. Use the approved test plan supplied by the user as the testing specification.

Do not modify anything yet.

Report:
1. Whether the validator exists and how it works.
2. Whether the pytest file is empty.
3. Whether the current Schema supports all ten approved test cases.
4. Whether the existing project dependencies support the tests.
5. Any CI or smoke-test dependencies that may be affected.

Wait for my approval before writing code. 
`````

505:     unresolved/failure groups separately; publish p50/p95, per-group sample counts, timeout counts
506:     and unconfirmed counts. Never count uncertain queue writes as confirmed or bypass human review
507:     to meet latency. The workload and numeric thresholds require Buna and PM ratification.

530: - id: REQ-COS-001
531:   type: cost
532:   title: Account for provider usage
533:   statement: The system shall account for provider-reported input and output tokens for each

535:     per-logical-request token budget shall trigger safe cost controls without granting the model

537:   fit_criterion: >-
538:     For a benchmark of at least 100 logical moderation requests under the recorded model/prompt
539:     configuration, target >= 95% of requests with fully reported usage at <= 4000 combined
540:     input+output tokens including retries; count unknown-usage requests separately and never
541:     exclude them silently from coverage. The provisional ADR-001-aligned per-invocation
542:     context envelope is 8192 tokens: up to 6144 input tokens, 1024 generated output tokens and
543:     1024 reserved tokens. Reserve is unused context headroom, not actual billed usage. Validate
544:     the context envelope before each model invocation; if it cannot be met, use a durably
545:     confirmed human-review hold (or truthfully report an unconfirmed handoff), with no automatic
546:     disposition. The separate total budget for a logical request including retries is OPEN
547:     for architecture, testing and PM review; 8192 must not be described as that aggregate cap. Verify
548:     normal, retry, missing-usage, budget-exhausted and provider-overrun fixtures; reconcile
549:     input/output per attempt and report complete-usage coverage. Buna and PM must ratify token
550:     targets and model assumptions. Additional provisional targets: median combined usage <= 2500
551:     tokens per logical request and p95 combined usage <= 4000 tokens among fully observed
552:     requests, measured with at least 100 fully observed logical requests. Target >= 95% complete
553:     provider usage reporting on the designated successful-request benchmark; report
554:     failed-provider attempts and their unknown usage in a separate count and denominator, never as
555:     zero tokens. Limit the planned provider-call retry policy to at most 2 total invocation
556:     attempts per logical request before safe hold; do not retry if the per-invocation envelope cannot be met or the pending aggregate
557:     request-budget policy prevents another attempt. Shiyi (Architecture Lead), Buna (Testing Lead)
558:     and PM must ratify these targets before any value becomes a release gate.

### Tool call: `read` — `call_f6425854c054414d894e36e3`

**Input**

  "filePath": "/mnt/c/Users/Mei HAN/Desktop/contentguard/scripts/validate_requirements.py"

**Output**

`````text
<path>/mnt/c/Users/Mei HAN/Desktop/contentguard/scripts/validate_requirements.py</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: """CI gate 3: the requirements registry must validate against its JSON schema.
2: 
3: Validates docs/req/requirements.yaml against docs/req/req-schema.json (D1) and
4: applies cross-field checks that JSON Schema cannot express: unique IDs,
5: ID-prefix/type agreement, and invariant safety fields.
6: Exit 0 = pass, 1 = fail (blocking).
7: """
8: 
9: import json
10: import re
11: import sys
12: from pathlib import Path
13: from typing import Any
14: 
15: import jsonschema  # type: ignore[import-untyped]
16: import yaml  # type: ignore[import-untyped]
17: 
18: ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
19: REGISTRY = ROOT / "docs" / "req" / "requirements.yaml"
20: SCHEMA = ROOT / "docs" / "req" / "req-schema.json"
21: 
22: ID_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^REQ-(FUN|INV|QUA|PER|COS|GOV|SEC)-\d{3}$")
23: TYPE_BY_ID_PREFIX = {
24:     "FUN": "functional",
25:     "INV": "invariant",
26:     "QUA": "quality",
27:     "PER": "performance",
28:     "COS": "cost",
29:     "GOV": "governance",
30:     "SEC": "security",
31: }
32: INVARIANT_STRING_FIELDS = ("enforcing_component", "fallback")
33: 
34: 
35: def _format_path(parts: Any) -> str:
36:     return "/".join(str(part) for part in parts) or "<root>"
37: 
38: 
39: def _schema_errors(data: Any, schema: Any) -> list[str]:
40:     validator = jsonschema.Draft202012Validator(
41:         schema, format_checker=jsonschema.FormatChecker()
42:     )
43:     return [
44:         f"registry invalid at '{_format_path(exc.absolute_path)}': {exc.message}"
45:         for exc in validator.iter_errors(data)
46:     ]
47: 
48: 
49: def _requirements(data: Any) -> list[Any]:
50:     if not isinstance(data, dict):
51:         return []
52:     requirements = data.get("requirements")
53:     return requirements if isinstance(requirements, list) else []
54: 
55: 
56: def _duplicate_id_errors(requirements: list[Any]) -> list[str]:
57:     errors: list[str] = []
58:     first_seen: dict[str, int] = {}
59:     for index, requirement in enumerate(requirements):
60:         if not isinstance(requirement, dict):
61:             continue
62:         req_id = requirement.get("id")
63:         if not isinstance(req_id, str):
64:             continue
65:         if req_id in first_seen:
66:             errors.append(
67:                 f"duplicate requirement id '{req_id}' at requirements[{index}] "
68:                 f"(first declared at requirements[{first_seen[req_id]}])"
69:             )
70:         else:
71:             first_seen[req_id] = index
72:     return errors
73: 
74: 
75: def _id_type_errors(requirements: list[Any]) -> list[str]:
76:     errors: list[str] = []
77:     for index, requirement in enumerate(requirements):
78:         if not isinstance(requirement, dict):
79:             continue
80:         req_id = requirement.get("id")
81:         req_type = requirement.get("type")
82:         if not isinstance(req_id, str) or not isinstance(req_type, str):
83:             continue
84:         match = ID_PATTERN.fullmatch(req_id)
85:         if match is None:
86:             continue
87:         expected_type = TYPE_BY_ID_PREFIX[match.group(1)]
88:         if req_type != expected_type:
89:             errors.append(
90:                 f"requirement id '{req_id}' at requirements[{index}] implies type "
91:                 f"'{expected_type}', but declared type is '{req_type}'"
92:             )
93:     return errors
94: 
95: 
96: def _invariant_errors(requirements: list[Any]) -> list[str]:
97:     errors: list[str] = []
98:     for index, requirement in enumerate(requirements):
99:         if not isinstance(requirement, dict) or requirement.get("type") != "invariant":
100:             continue
101:         req_id = requirement.get("id")
102:         label = req_id if isinstance(req_id, str) else f"<requirements[{index}]>"
103:         for field in INVARIANT_STRING_FIELDS:
104:             value = requirement.get(field)
105:             if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip():
106:                 errors.append(
107:                     f"invariant '{label}' at requirements[{index}] must declare a "
108:                     f"non-empty '{field}'"
109:                 )
110:         if requirement.get("zero_tolerance") is not True:
111:             errors.append(
112:                 f"invariant '{label}' at requirements[{index}] must set "
113:                 "'zero_tolerance: true'"
114:             )
115:     return errors
116: 
117: 
118: def _semantic_errors(requirements: list[Any]) -> list[str]:
119:     return [
120:         *_duplicate_id_errors(requirements),
121:         *_id_type_errors(requirements),
122:         *_invariant_errors(requirements),
123:     ]
124: 
125: 
126: def main() -> int:
127:     if not REGISTRY.exists() or not SCHEMA.exists():
128:         print("GATE 3 FAIL: registry or schema file is missing")
129:         return 1
130:     try:
131:         data = yaml.safe_load(REGISTRY.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
132:         schema = json.loads(SCHEMA.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
133:     except (OSError, yaml.YAMLError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
134:         print(f"GATE 3 FAIL: could not read or parse registry or schema: {exc}")
135:         return 1
136: 
137:     try:

<content>
1: # Review Memos (D4) — one memo per pull request
2: 
3: > Each PR gets a memo covering: spec alignment, architecture alignment, code quality,
4: > test quality, operational metadata — security and efficiency checked throughout —
5: > ending in a verdict: **APPROVE / CHANGES REQUIRED / REJECT**.
6: 
7: ## Review process (CR assignment system, team proposal §4.2, agreed 2026-11-15)
8: 
9: 1. **Domain-assigned CR:** every PR is reviewed by the **non-author owner of the
10:    matching main line** — architecture PRs → architecture owner; test PRs → testing
11:    owner; governance/CI PRs → governance & CI owner; requirements PRs → requirements
12:    owner. (Not a rotation: the domain expert reviews, so review-memo content stays
13:    consistent with each deliverable line.)
14: 2. **PM gate:** the PM provides the memo template, **signs off every memo**, and
15:    personally reviews critical PRs (invariants, architecture, anything touching D7
16:    gates or the contribution method).
17: 3. **Safety-critical paths** (`src/contentguard/policy/**`, files named in
18:    `docs/architecture/invariants.md`): the **architecture owner co-signs** (D7
19:    hard requirement) **and** a linked architecture decision (ADR) is mandatory —
20:    branch protection enforces human review on these paths (§6).
21: 4. PRs stay small (one feature/invariant slice); memos are written **before** merge.
22: 5. Review turnaround target: 24 h. A PR waiting longer is escalated at the daily
23:    stand-up (risk RSK-3).
24: 6. **An approval is a judgement, not a click.** Review from your own main-line role:
25:    state what you actually checked and whether the change is *right*, not merely that it
26:    exists. If it is wrong or incomplete, do not approve — record the concrete problems and
27:    return CHANGES REQUIRED. A verdict with no check behind it is noise, and a four-minute
28:    bare approve is treated as no review at all. What each role is expected to check:
29:    - **Requirements owner:** requirement IDs, fit criteria, priority (MoSCoW) correctness,
30:      traceability from each requirement to a component/test.
31:    - **Architecture owner:** ADR conformance, module/authority boundaries, layered context
32:      structure, token budget and compaction thresholds.
33:    - **Testing owner:** negative test per invariant, threshold/eligibility of the gates,
34:      offline-vs-live separation.
35:    - **Governance & CI owner:** that the document's claims match machine behaviour —
36:      branch protection, CODEOWNERS resolution, gate semantics — and that red-line
37:      boundaries (agent-generated code, human-owned files) are respected.
38:    - **PM:** spec alignment, cross-artifact consistency, and the contribution/process
39:      record.
40: 
41: ## Memo template (copy per PR)
42: 
43: ```
44: ## RM-<NNN> — PR #<n>: <title>
45: 
46: - Reviewer(s): domain CR <name>; PM <name>; architecture co-sign <name> (safety-critical only)
47: - Spec alignment: <requirement IDs touched; registry updated? MoSCoW respected?>

56: 
57: ## PR & commit conventions (agreed 2026-11-15)
58: 
59: - **One logical change per PR** — a feature slice or a single deliverable chunk;
60:   small PRs keep memos meaningful.
61: - **Branch:** `<type>/<req-or-inv-id>-<slug>` (e.g. `feat/REQ-FUN-001-intake`).
62: - **PR title:** `<type>(<scope>): <subject>` — cite REQ/INV IDs in title or body;
63:   squash merge appends `(#<n>)`. Template: `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`;
64:   test plans go through `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/test-plan.md` issues (gate 1).
65: - **Merge method: squash only** (repo setting) — `main` becomes a curated ledger of
66:   one commit per reviewed change; granular agent-session commits stay visible on the
67:   PR page (D5 evidence). Consistent with the required linear history.
68: - **Contribution attribution (D6) rests on three anchors**, not on squash-commit
69:   authorship: (1) `docs/ai-use-log.yaml` `operator` + `pr` fields, (2) the PR body
70:   (authored by the member), (3) the man-hours table in `docs/contribution-log.md`.
71:   Authors merge their own PRs after approval — a responsibility discipline, not the
72:   attribution mechanism.
73: - **Prologue (history chapter 0):** the first 10 commits (the 2026-11-15 bootstrap
74:   cohort) were direct pushes while branch protection was being set up. They contain
75:   human-written docs/governance files (red-line compliant) **and** the human-drafted
76:   scaffold seed (CI workflow, `scripts/**`, `prompts/**`, test scaffolding incl.
77:   `test_smoke.py`, `pytest.ini`) written before the team adopted the agentic red
78:   line — tracked as **TD-3** in `docs/debt-register.md`, to be regenerated through
79:   OpenCode sessions. Two follow-up governance fixes also landed via admin windows
80:   (roster re-key; cross-session audit fixes) — recorded here as exceptions. From
81:   now on **every change lands via PR**; an exceptional admin window must carry an
82:   `[admin-window]` tag in the commit message **and** be recorded in the next review
83:   memo.
84: 
85: ## Memos
86: 
87: > RM-N = the Nth memo in this ledger, ordered by the intended PR sequence.
88: > RM-002/RM-003 were backfilled by PR #9 after RM-004 was drafted in PR #8;
89: > they are placed before RM-004 so the ledger reads in PR order. A dismissed
90: > "RM-003" review draft on PR #5 predates this ledger's RM-003 and is void.
91: >
92: > **2026-11-16 ledger finalization note (supersedes the ordering rule above for
93: > RM-005 onward).** Reviewers posted memos in PR threads faster than the ledger could

def _registry_fixture(tmp_path: Path, mutate: Callable[[dict[str, Any]], None] | None = None, *, registry_text: str | None = None, schema_text: str | None = None) -> tuple[Path, Path]:

    schema.write_text(schema_text if schema_text is not None else SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), encoding="utf-8")
    return registry, schema

```
def _run(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
    registry: Path,
    schema: Path,
) -> tuple[int, str]:
    monkeypatch.setattr(validator, "REGISTRY", registry)
    monkeypatch.setattr(validator, "SCHEMA", schema)
    exit_code = validator.main()
    return exit_code, capsys.readouterr().out
```

"""Gate 3 requirements-validator tests (deterministic, offline).

Every case drives scripts/validate_requirements.py through temporary fixtures and
cites the governance requirement it verifies (REQ-GOV-003 and/or REQ-GOV-004).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import importlib.util
from pathlib import Path
from types import ModuleType
from typing import Any

import pytest
import yaml

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
VALIDATOR_PATH = ROOT / "scripts" / "validate_requirements.py"
SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / "docs" / "req" / "req-schema.json"

TRADEOFF_NOTE = (
    "MVP includes content intake, advisory analysis, deterministic enforcement, "
    "human review, durable audit, and six blocking CI governance gates."
)


def _load_validator() -> ModuleType:
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
        "validate_requirements_under_test", VALIDATOR_PATH
    )
    assert spec is not None and spec.loader is not None
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


validator = _load_validator()


def _valid_registry_data() -> dict[str, Any]:
    return {
        "project": "ContentGuard",
        "version": "1.0",
        "moscow_tradeoff_note": TRADEOFF_NOTE,
        "requirements": [
            {
                "id": "REQ-GOV-003",
                "type": "governance",
                "title": "Validate the registry",
                "statement": (
                    "The registry shall pass validation against the repository "
                    "requirement schema before merge."
                ),
                "fit_criterion": (
                    "Gate 3 accepts valid registry data and rejects invalid "
                    "entries with clear diagnostics."
                ),
                "priority": "must",
                "status": "baselined",
                "changed_date": "2026-11-16",
                "verification": {
                    "method": "ci-gate",
                    "evidence": "scripts/validate_requirements.py",
                },
                "source": {
                    "origin": "Course D1 and repo workflow gate 3",
                    "links": ["docs/req/req-schema.json"],
                },
            },
            {
                "id": "REQ-INV-001",
                "type": "invariant",
                "title": "AI output never executes enforcement",
                "statement": (
                    "Only the deterministic policy engine shall authorize and "
                    "effect consequential moderation actions."
                ),
                "fit_criterion": (
                    "Attempt a forged model action and assert no protected "
                    "state change or external effect."
                ),
                "priority": "must",
                "status": "baselined",
                "changed_date": "2026-11-16",
                "verification": {
                    "method": "structural-check",
                    "evidence": "scripts/validate_requirements.py",
                },
                "source": {
                    "origin": "Team kickoff discussion 2026-11-15",
                    "links": ["docs/architecture/invariants.md"],
                },
                "enforcing_component": "policy engine (deterministic)",
                "fallback": (
                    "Block merge on a boundary bypass and disable the affected "
                    "enforcement path."
                ),
                "zero_tolerance": True,
            },
        ],
    }


def _fixture(
    tmp_path: Path, data: dict[str, Any], name: str
) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    registry = tmp_path / f"{name}-requirements.yaml"
    schema = tmp_path / f"{name}-req-schema.json"
    registry.write_text(
        yaml.safe_dump(data, sort_keys=False, allow_unicode=True), encoding="utf-8"
    )
    schema.write_text(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), encoding="utf-8")
    return registry, schema


def _run(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
    registry: Path,
    schema: Path,
) -> tuple[int, str]:
    monkeypatch.setattr(validator, "REGISTRY", registry)
    monkeypatch.setattr(validator, "SCHEMA", schema)
    exit_code = validator.main()
    return exit_code, capsys.readouterr().out


def test_duplicate_id_rejected(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003/REQ-GOV-004: duplicate requirement IDs must be rejected."""
    data = _valid_registry_data()
    data["requirements"].append(copy.deepcopy(data["requirements"][0]))
    registry, schema = _fixture(tmp_path, data, "duplicate-id")
    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code != 0
    assert "duplicate requirement id 'REQ-GOV-003'" in output


def test_id_type_agreement(

) -> None:

    assert "declared type is 'functional'" in output

def test_unknown_type_rejected(
    tmp_path: Path,

def test_moscow_tradeoff_note_required(

    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,

```python
"""Gate 3 requirements-validator tests (deterministic, offline).

Every case drives scripts/validate_requirements.py through temporary fixtures and
cites the governance requirement it verifies (REQ-GOV-003 and/or REQ-GOV-004).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import importlib.util
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
from types import ModuleType
from typing import Any

import pytest
import yaml

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
VALIDATOR_PATH = ROOT / "scripts" / "validate_requirements.py"
SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / "docs" / "req" / "req-schema.json"
REGISTRY_PATH = ROOT / "docs" / "req" / "requirements.yaml"


def _load_validator() -> ModuleType:
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
        "validate_requirements_under_test", VALIDATOR_PATH
    )
    assert spec is not None and spec.loader is not None
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


validator = _load_validator()


def _valid_registry_data() -> dict[str, Any]:
    data = yaml.safe_load(REGISTRY_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    assert isinstance(data, dict)
    requirements = data.get("requirements")
    assert isinstance(requirements, list) and len(requirements) == 31
    return data


def _fixture(
    tmp_path: Path, data: dict[str, Any], name: str
) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    registry = tmp_path / f"{name}-requirements.yaml"
    schema = tmp_path / f"{name}-req-schema.json"
    registry.write_text(
        yaml.safe_dump(data, sort_keys=False, allow_unicode=True), encoding="utf-8"
    )
    schema.write_text(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), encoding="utf-8")
    return registry, schema


def _copy_fixture(tmp_path: Path, name: str) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    registry = tmp_path / f"{name}-requirements.yaml"
    schema = tmp_path / f"{name}-req-schema.json"
    shutil.copyfile(REGISTRY_PATH, registry)
    shutil.copyfile(SCHEMA_PATH, schema)
    return registry, schema


def _run(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
    registry: Path,
    schema: Path,
) -> tuple[int, str]:
    monkeypatch.setattr(validator, "REGISTRY", registry)
    monkeypatch.setattr(validator, "SCHEMA", schema)
    exit_code = validator.main()
    return exit_code, capsys.readouterr().out


def test_duplicate_id_rejected(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003/REQ-GOV-004: duplicate requirement IDs must be rejected."""
    data = _valid_registry_data()
    data["requirements"].append(copy.deepcopy(data["requirements"][0]))
    registry, schema = _fixture(tmp_path, data, "duplicate-id")
    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code != 0
    assert "duplicate requirement id 'REQ-INV-001'" in output


def test_id_type_agreement(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003/REQ-GOV-004: an ID prefix must agree with the declared type."""
    data = _valid_registry_data()
    data["requirements"][0]["type"] = "functional"
    registry, schema = _fixture(tmp_path, data, "id-type")
    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code != 0
    assert "implies type 'invariant'" in output
    assert "declared type is 'functional'" in output


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "missing_field",
    ["enforcing_component", "fallback", "zero_tolerance"],
)
def test_invariant_required_fields(
    missing_field: str,
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003: every invariant must declare all mandatory safety fields."""
    data = _valid_registry_data()
    data["requirements"][0].pop(missing_field)
    registry, schema = _fixture(tmp_path, data, f"invariant-fields-{missing_field}")
    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code != 0
    assert missing_field in output
    assert "REQ-INV-001" in output


def test_lifecycle_status_values(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003: an invalid lifecycle status must be rejected."""
    data = _valid_registry_data()
    data["requirements"][0]["status"] = "approved"
    registry, schema = _fixture(tmp_path, data, "lifecycle-status")
    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code != 0
    assert "'approved' is not one of" in output
    assert "status" in output


def test_valid_registry_passes(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003/REQ-GOV-004: the actual 31-requirement registry must pass gate 3."""
    registry, schema = _copy_fixture(tmp_path, "valid")
    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code == 0
    assert "GATE 3 PASS: 31 requirements valid against schema" in output


def test_missing_fallback_rejected(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003: an invariant without a concrete fallback must be rejected."""

    data["requirements"][1].pop("fallback")

    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)
    assert exit_code != 0
    assert "'fallback' is a required property" in output
    assert "non-empty 'fallback'" in output


def test_unknown_type_rejected(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:

    exit_code, output = _run(monkeypatch, capsys, registry, schema)

def test_moscow_tradeoff_note_required(
    tmp_path: Path,
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
) -> None:
    """REQ-GOV-003: the MoSCoW note must exist and meet its minimum length."""
    missing = _valid_registry_data()

    registry, schema = _fixture(tmp_path, missing, "note-missing")

### Coverage of the ten approved cases

| # | Test name | Coverage |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `test_duplicate_id_rejected` | Appends a deep copy of the first real requirement (`REQ-INV-001`) to the 31-requirement registry; asserts non-zero exit and the duplicate-ID diagnostic. |
| 2 | `test_id_type_agreement` | Changes the first real requirement’s `type` to `functional`; asserts non-zero exit and the `invariant`/`functional` mismatch message. Docstring now cites REQ-GOV-003 and REQ-GOV-004. |
| 3 | `test_invariant_required_fields` | Parameterized over `enforcing_component`, `fallback`, and `zero_tolerance`; each is removed from the first real invariant and must be rejected with the field name and `REQ-INV-001` in the output. The separate `test_missing_fallback_rejected` remains. |
| 4 | `test_lifecycle_status_values` | Sets the first real requirement’s `status` to invalid value `approved`; asserts non-zero exit and enum rejection message. |
| 5 | `test_valid_registry_passes` | Uses `_copy_fixture` with `shutil.copyfile` to copy the exact real YAML and schema into `tmp_path`; asserts exit `0` and the exact `GATE 3 PASS: 31 requirements valid against schema` message. Docstring cites both IDs. |
| 6 | `test_missing_fallback_rejected` | Removes `fallback` from the second real invariant; asserts both schema-level and semantic fallback rejection messages. |
| 7 | `test_unknown_type_rejected` | Sets the first real requirement’s `type` to `unknown`; asserts non-zero exit and enum rejection. |
| 8 | `test_moscow_tradeoff_note_required` | Covers both required variants: missing root note and a <40-character note; each asserts non-zero exit and the corresponding required/minLength message. |
| 9 | `test_source_origin_required` | Removes `source.origin` from the first real requirement; asserts non-zero exit and required-property message. Docstring now cites both IDs. |
| 10 | `test_invariant_zero_tolerance_must_be_true` | Sets the second real invariant’s `zero_tolerance` to `False`; asserts non-zero exit plus schema const and semantic messages. |

All ten approved function names are preserved. Every fixture is under `tmp_path`; `monkeypatch` redirects the validator’s `REGISTRY`/`SCHEMA` constants, and real schema/registry files are read-only. Awaiting your review.

ORDER BY time_created, id\",\n    (sid,),\n).fetchall()\nparts = con.execute(\n    \"SELECT id, message_id, data, time_created FROM part WHERE session_id=? ORDER BY time_created, id\",\n    (sid,),\n).fetchall()\nparts_by_message = defaultdict(list)\nfor part in parts:\n    parts_by_message[part[\"message_id\"]].append(part)\n\n\ndef stamp(ms: int) -> str:\n    return datetime.fromtimestamp(ms / 1000, tz=timezone.utc).isoformat()\n\n\ndef block(text: str, label: str = \"text\") -> list[str]:\n    return [f\"`````{label}\", text, \"`````\", \"\"]\n\n\nlines: list[str] = []\nlines += [\n    \"# Gate 3 Validator v2 — OpenCode session transcript\",\n    \"\",\n    \"Verbatim export of the stored OpenCode session. No messages were reconstructed or invented.\",\n    \"\",\n    f\"- Session ID: `{session['id']}`\",\n    f\"- Slug: `{session['slug']}`\",\n    f\"- Title: {session['title']}\",\n    f\"- Working directory: `{session['directory']}`\",\n    f\"- Agent: `{session['agent']}`\",\n    f\"- Model: `{session['model']}`\",\n    f\"- Created (UTC): {stamp(session['time_created'])}\",\n    f\"- Last updated (UTC): {stamp(session['time_updated'])}\",\n    f\"- Export source: `{db}` (read-only query of `session`, `message`, and `part` tables)\",\n    f\"- Messages exported: {len(messages)}\",\n    f\"- Parts exported: {len(parts)}\",\n    \"\",\n    \"Tool inputs and outputs below are the actual stored `part` payloads; long file reads and command outputs are preserved as captured.\",\n    \"\",\n    \"---\",\n    \"\",\n]\n\nfor msg in messages:\n    meta = json.loads(msg[\"data\"])\n    role = meta.get(\"role\", \"unknown\")\n    lines += [f\"## {stamp(msg['time_created'])} — {role} — `{msg['id']}`\", \"\"]\n    for part in parts_by_message[msg[\"id\"]]:\n        payload = json.loads(part[\"data\"])\n        ptype = payload.get(\"type\", \"unknown\")\n        if ptype == \"text\":\n            lines += block(payload.get(\"text\", \"\"), \"text\")\n        elif ptype == \"reasoning\":\n            lines += [\"### Reasoning\", \"\"] + block(payload.get(\"text\", \"\"), \"text\")\n        elif ptype == \"tool\":\n            state = payload.get(\"state\", {})\n            lines += [f\"### Tool call: `{payload.get('tool')}` — `{payload.get('callID')}`\", \"\"]\n            lines += [\"**Input**\", \"\"] + block(\n                json.dumps(state.get(\"input\"), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), \"json\"\n            )\n            rendered_output = state.get(\"output\")\n            if not isinstance(rendered_output, str):\n                rendered_output = json.dumps(rendered_output, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\n            lines += [\"**Output**\", \"\"] + block(rendered_output, \"text\")\n        else:\n            lines += [f\"### Part type: `{ptype}`\", \"\"] + block(\n                json.dumps(payload, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), \"json\"\n            )\n    lines += [\"---\", \"\"]\n\nlines += [\"# Appendix — stored message metadata\", \"\"]\nfor msg in messages:\n    lines += [f\"## `{msg['id']}`\", \"\"] + block(\n        json.dumps(json.loads(msg[\"data\"]), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), \"json\"\n    )\n\nout.write_text(\"\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\nprint(f\"wrote {out} ({out.stat().st_size} bytes, {len(messages)} messages, {len(parts)} parts)\")\nPY",
  "workdir": "/mnt/c/Users/Mei HAN/Desktop/contentguard"
}
